# Seven boundary points: every 3/5 OP line when h>=e

This note closes the entire single-exterior N7 3/5 OP-line class in
the height chamber h>=e. The positive three-vertex arc may occupy any
position in the actual octagon. The HGF position is already excluded
at every height by N7_THREE_FIVE_TOP.md; this note covers the other
seven positions by 71 fixed cubic sums and two direct sign contradictions.

The 22,579 positive integer terms are printed in full. Their factors,
range constants, sign choices, and conditional occupancy witnesses are
specified before the sums. Exact rational coefficient bounds make each
sum negative. The optional standard-library checker
`check_n7_three_five_upper_height.py` reads the printed manuscript and
reconstructs all factors. Neither a search nor an optimizer is a premise.

Throughout, areas are doubled and

    s=93/1000,  Gamma2=4+2sqrt(3),  K=149/20<Gamma2.

## 1. Statement, projected boundary facts, and the ordinate bound

Use the actual points

    A=(0,a), B=(b,0), C=(c,0), D=(1,d),
    E=(1,e), F=(f,1), G=(g,z), H=(0,h),
    O=(x,y), P=(u,v).

The normalization is that of N7_OP_LINE_CAPS.md: the seven contacts
A,B,C,D,E,F,H lie on their indicated square sides, G is in the upper-left
exterior cap, and O,P are strictly inside the contact heptagon. In
particular,

    0<a<h<1, 0<b<c<1, 0<d<e<1,
    b<=f<=c, d<=h, a<=e, 0<g<f, h<z<1.

The actual hull is the strictly convex octagon A,B,C,D,E,F,G,H.
Every actual unsigned triangle determinant is at least s. This note
assumes h>=e. Project G only to derive inequalities, writing G'=(g,1).
Then

    FG'H=(f-g)(1-h)>=FGH>=s,
    EFG'=(f-g)(1-e)>=(f-g)(1-h)>=s.

The other six boundary ears are unchanged. Thus all eight projected
boundary-ear inequalities hold. The boundary-only results of
N8_BOUNDARY_RANGES.md give, with

    t=31/251,  o=31657/55000,  q=1-t,
    M=753/1000,  delta=21/250,

that every projected corner offset belongs to [t,o], every projected
side span belongs to [s/o,M], and

    e-a,h-d,f-b,c-g>=delta.                          (1)

The actual side pairs AH,DE,BC consequently give

    t<=x,u<=q,       y,v>=t.                         (2)

We also have the uniform upper ordinate bound

    y,v<=q.                                        (3)

To prove it, let T=(X,Y) be either interior point. If X<=f, raising
G increases the actual determinant FGT by (1-z)(f-X). Therefore
(f-g)(1-Y)=FG'T>=s, and f-g<=M gives 1-Y>=s/M=t.
If X>=f, the actual upper-edge clearance is

    EFT=(1-f)(1-Y)-(1-e)(X-f)>=s.

It gives 1-Y>=s/(1-f)>=s/o>t. These two closed alternatives cover
every X, including X=f. This argument uses no unproved triangle
inequality involving a projected point.

All eight square-symmetry images of the N8 long-triangle cap estimate
also hold for the following actual triangles:

    AEF, BEF, ABE, ABF, CDG, CGH, CDH, DGH <69/100.   (4)

For triangles not involving G the area is unchanged by projection.
For CDG,CGH,DGH it increases with z, with respective coefficients
1-c,c,1. Hence each actual area is at most its projected counterpart,
to which N8_TRIANGLE_CAPS.md applies using the eight ear inequalities.
Because Gamma2*s>69/100, each triangle in (4) has capacity one for
O,P. N7_OP_LINE_CAPS.md supplies the same capacity for all eight
consecutive ears and all eight four-consecutive-vertex caps.

## 2. The complete arc and sign catalogue

For a hull vertex I write d_I=IOP=det(P-O,I-O). Orient OP so that
the shorter arc is positive. The eight possible positive triples are

    ABC, BCD, CDE, DEF, EFG, FGH, GHA, HAB.

The FGH case is already excluded by N7_THREE_FIVE_TOP.md. For a
remaining arc I1,I2,I3, let I0 be its preceding hull vertex and I4
its following vertex. Convexity puts O,P in the five-vertex cap
I0,I1,I2,I3,I4 and on the inner side of the chord I1I3. They are also
inside every positive edge half-plane of the contact heptagon.

Every four-consecutive cap I,J,K,L is on the negative side of IL.
The identity

    ILP-ILO=d_I-d_L                                 (5)

and its capacity one give two useful forced signs: if d_I<0<d_L,
then ILO>0; if d_I>0>d_L, then ILP>0. For example, a negative
earlier value in the first case would make both values negative,
putting both points in that cap.

The following common sign charts record these implications. Each
chart gives the signs of IJO,IJP for every hull pair IJ. A question
mark denotes an undecided sign. Every cyclic hull triple is positive;
the eight OP signs are fixed by the named positive arc.

The finite case tree is easiest to specify once. Its ten formal
leaves are:

| Leaf | Formal successive choices |
|---|---|
| 1 | BHO+ |
| 2 | BHO-,DHO- |
| 3 | BHO-,DHO+,EHP+ |
| 4 | BHO-,DHO+,EHP-,AGO+ |
| 5 | BHO-,DHO+,EHP-,AGO-,BGO-,CHO+ |
| 6 | BHO-,DHO+,EHP-,AGO-,BGO-,CHO-,CFP+ |
| 7 | BHO-,DHO+,EHP-,AGO-,BGO-,CHO-,CFP- |
| 8 | BHO-,DHO+,EHP-,AGO-,BGO+,CFP- |
| 9 | BHO-,DHO+,EHP-,AGO-,BGO+,CFP+,BFP+ |
| 10 | BHO-,DHO+,EHP-,AGO-,BGO+,CFP+,BFP- |

This is an exhaustive binary tree because every listed determinant is
an actual triangle and cannot vanish. To specify its choices at every
arc, index A,...,H by 0,...,7 and let r be the arc's starting index.
For odd r relabel a hull index i as i+r-5 modulo 8 and leave O,P
unchanged. For even r relabel i as r+7-i modulo 8, exchange the
names O,P, and reverse the sign of every determinant. Reordering a
triangle's letters then uses the usual alternating determinant sign.

These are bijective substitutions in a binary list of sign choices.
They make no claim that a square symmetry preserves the missing
contact or the h>=e condition: every resulting factor is evaluated in
the original coordinates of Section 1. The explicit ledger below
removes any possible ambiguity in the substitutions. In arcs BCD,CDE,
GHA, split formal leaf 3 once more by the transported BGO sign.

Two formal leaves contradict a positive heptagon-edge determinant
immediately: DEF1 requires FHO<0, and GHA4 requires FHP<0. The
remaining ledger consists of exactly 71 polynomial rows.

Here and below, the only rules used to propagate actual signs are:

1. A containing triangle fixes every chord side on which all its
   vertices lie. Three positive cyclic edge tests certify containment.
2. Equation (5), for any hull chord with opposite endpoint OP signs,
   propagates a positive earlier or negative later value in its
   monotone direction.
3. If one point lies in a proved capacity-one polygon and all but one
   edge tests for the other point are positive, its last test is negative.

The capacity-one polygons are exactly those listed at the end of
Section 1. Applying these rules also to a row's choices gives the
following additional actual signs. No extra orientation choices are
introduced by this table.

## 3. The two rational boxes and affine factors

Use coordinate order (a,b,c,d,e,f,g,h,z,x,y,u,v). Put

    eta=93/790, ell=7347/69700, r0=1-s,
    X=s/(203/250-eta),
    F0=K*s/(1-eta), E0=eta+s/(1-F0).

Type N is the normal projected-boundary box. Type T is used when
the sign tests put one of O,P inside AGH. Its validity follows from
the support and fan proof in N7_THREE_FIVE_TOP.md Section 3, which
depends only on that containment, not on the particular OP arc.
Specifically, AGH>=3s and FGH>=s give f(1-a)>=K*s and

    (1-ell)(h-eta)(1-h)-3s(1-h)-s(h-eta)>=0.

The exact quadratic calculation there gives 257/500<h<203/250.
Intersecting its other proved bounds with (1)-(3) gives the two boxes
below. All comparisons between the displayed rational constants are
exact.

| Coordinate | Type N lower | Type N upper | Type T lower | Type T upper |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| a | t | o | t | 1-K*s |
| b | t | o | X | o |
| c | 1-o | q | F0 | q |
| d | t | o | t | o |
| e | 1-o | q | E0 | q |
| f | 1-o | q | F0 | q |
| g | t | o | 3X | o |
| h | 1-o | q | 257/500 | 203/250 |
| z | 1-o+s | 1 | 257/500+s | 1 |
| x,u | t | q | X | q |
| y,v | t | q or r0 as recorded below | t | q or r0 as recorded below |

Both possible ordinate caps are valid: (3) proves the stronger q,
and q<r0. Some sums use the weaker r0 for their affine constants.
The next ledger records these choices, the box type, and the contained
point that justifies Type T. For Type N put X0=t; for Type T put X0=X.

These are differences of two opposite-sign OP determinants at the
corresponding left, right, or bottom side pair, divided by the proved
side-span upper bound. The constants 2ell and 2eta are permissible
weakenings of 2t. In Type T, if the AGH occupant is T=(X_T,Y_T),
append next g-X_T-2X0: its two nonbase fan triangles in AGH give
(h-a)(g-X_T)>=2s. Finally append h-e and c-g-delta, in that order.
Continue numbering L38,L39,... along this explicit list. Thus every
factor index in every row is unambiguous, including the rows with
an extra AGH fan factor.

## 4. Area, endpoint, occupancy, and spread factors

The notation XYZ+ means XYZ-s and XYZ- means -XYZ-s, using the
actual signs in Section 2. The following common endpoint factors
are proved in N7_OP_LINE_CAPS.md Sections 5-6:

    EP=f(1-a)-4s, EL=hc-4s, ER=e(1-b)-4s,
    SUP=f^2(z-a)-fg(1-a)-4s(f-g),
    P_AHGFE=f(1-e)+(1-f)(1-a-11s),
    P_BCDEF=e(1-f)+(1-e)(1-b-11s),
    P_DEFGH=g(h-d)+z-h-11sg,
    P_HABCD=(h-11s)(1-c)+dc,
    P_ABCDE=(e-11s)b+a(1-b).

There are three conditional strengthenings. If at least one of ABC,
ABH contains an interior point, set ELK=hc-K*s. If at least one of
BCD,CDE contains one, set ERK=e(1-b)-K*s. If AGH or FGH contains
one, set

    SUPK=f^2(z-a)-fg(1-a)-K*s(f-g).

Each follows by applying the paired Cauchy inequality from
N7_OP_LINE_CAPS.md Section 2 to one area at least 3s and the
other at least s. The container area is at least Gamma2*s>K*s.
Every use of these conditional factors has a witness below.

The global upper-area factors from the same note are

    UABCD=8s-area(ABCD), UCDEF=8s-area(CDEF),
    UEFGH=8s-area(EFGH), UGHAB=8s-area(GHAB),
    UBR=9s-e(1-b),
    UTR=9s(f-g)-(1-g)[(1-d)(f-g)+(1-z)(1-f)].

For every three-letter word in (4), UXYZ means 69/100-XYZ;
Section 1 proves it nonnegative in the present height chamber.

Conditional interior factors use the same definitions as
N7_THREE_FIVE_TOP.md:

    CXYZ_1=XYZ-3s,
    CXYZ_2=XYZ-K*s,
    QW=area(W)-8s,
    RW_IJ=area(W)-(29/5)s-sign(IJO)*(IJO-IJP).

CXYZ_j requires at least j of O,P inside the actual cyclic triangle
XYZ. QW requires both inside the actual cyclic quadrilateral W.
RW_IJ additionally requires both diagonals of W to separate O,P;
IJ is one of those diagonals. The fan and triangle-occupancy bounds,
the quadrilateral 8s bound, and the threshold-free spread inequality
of QUADRILATERAL_SPREAD.md prove these factors nonnegative.

For a fixed row, let W be its displayed sum of positive integer
weights times products of these factors. Thus W>=0. Expand it as

    W=sum_alpha c_alpha product_i w_i^alpha_i.

Since all coordinates are nonnegative, the row's exact rational box
gives

    W<=B(W):=sum_(c_alpha>0)c_alpha product_i hi_i^alpha_i
              +sum_(c_alpha<0)c_alpha product_i lo_i^alpha_i.

Every exact value below is negative. This contradicts W>=0 in each
of the 71 rows. The two other leaves were contradicted directly in
Section 2, and the FGH arc was closed in N7_THREE_FIVE_TOP.md.
Hence every 3/5 OP-line position is excluded when h>=e.

The h<e positions other than the already-closed FGH arc, the 4/4
OP-line cases, and the all-heptagon-interior N7 placement remain
separate obligations. No global N7 attainment claim is made here.

## 6. A reusable five-convex-point support inequality

We also record a scale-free consequence of the convex-five endpoint
theorem, including the sign of its supporting-line denominator.
Let A0,B0,C0,D0,E0 be any five actual points in strictly convex cyclic
order, and put

    X=A0B0E0,       Y=A0D0E0,       Z=A0B0D0.

All three areas are positive. Then

    X*Y-11s*(X-Z)>=0.                               (7)

If X-Z<=0, (7) is immediate. Suppose Delta=X-Z>0. The supporting
rays from A0 through B0 and from E0 through D0 meet at

    R=A0+(Y/Delta)(B0-A0)
     =E0+(X/Delta)(D0-E0).

These identities follow by taking the determinant of the two ray
directions, which is -Delta. Both ray parameters exceed one:
X-Delta=Z>0 and

    Y-Delta=Y-X+Z=B0D0E0>0.

Thus the meeting point is beyond B0 on the first ray and beyond D0
on the second. The convex five-point chain lies between its endpoint
chord A0E0 and these two supporting lines, so it lies in the triangle
A0,E0,R. That triangle has two actual vertices and doubled area
X*Y/Delta. The scale-free convex-five bound, proved in
N7_SAME_UPPER_CAP.md Section 1 and extended explicitly in
N7_OP_LINE_CAPS.md Section 6, requires this area to exceed 11s.
Multiplication by the positive Delta proves (7), with a strict
inequality in this alternative. The zero and negative denominator
alternatives were already covered, so there is no meeting-direction
exception to (7).

For the two remaining five-vertex chains of the inward-G octagon,
this gives in particular

    CFG*CDG-11s*(CDG-CDF)>=0,
    ABF*BFG-11s*(BFG-AFG)>=0.

These quartic consequences are independent reusable inequalities;
the cubic sums below use the explicitly listed factors of Section 4.
